Rubaylo on the trials of the launch system in 1956 | NASA SP-2006-4110 | The support booms came out of the missile one after another, tenths of a second apart, where the documents asked for all four together | The trials at the Leningrad Metal Works, before the pad was built |
| Cheremnykh, «Создание транспортного и заправочного оборудования для ракеты-носителя "Восток"», *Tekhnicheskie gazy*, 2011 No. 3, pp. 3-6 | the journal's PDF | **The railway fillers**: the oxygen filler 8G117 "about 25 m over the axes of its couplers", two vessels and a pump compartment, on four-axle bogies; the toppers 8G118 and 8G128 like the transport tanks 8G52 and 8G54, standing "under a protective canopy" through the launch; an artist's view of the trains at the pad; green | No drawing to scale; which side of the rocket each stood on is not said |
| Mikheev, in Golubkov and Mikheev, *Baikonur. Klyuch na start!* (Mariupol, 2014) | [proza.ru/2016/05/22/1305](https://proza.ru/2016/05/22/1305) | The kerosene filler 8G119: "an eight-axle car 24 metres long", two tanks of 50 m³; on 12 April 1961 "seven metres from the rocket"; with half an hour to go a rail tractor takes the fillers a kilometre off; the peroxide filler made to look like a passenger coach | A designer's memoir, fifty years on |
| The Novosti Kosmonavtiki forum, "Стартовый комплекс ракеты Союз" (topic 9350) | read by an earlier pass | That Gagarin's rocket was served by two cable masts and a cable and filling mast of the Molniya's kind, its head above the fairing; the low cable mast "a tube with a little ladder"; a service truss stands over the cable and filling mast and is drawn off before it | A forum: people who have studied the films, not a document |
| SRTM 1 arc-second, tile N45E063 (the Shuttle Radar Topography Mission, February 2000; heights above the EGM96 geoid), read on its own grid for 560 m round the pad | [AWS Terrain Tiles, skadi/N45/N45E063.hgt.gz](https://s3.amazonaws.com/elevation-tiles-prod/skadi/N45/N45E063.hgt.gz) | **The lie of the land round Site 1** (places/tyuratam/site1/relief.py): 113 to 114 m along the tracks from 450 m west to the pad; 112 to 117 m from 60 to 180 m south of them; 104 to 107 m from 65 m to 400 m north; 99 to 103 m north-west of the fence; 107 to 109 m east of the pit's point; the spoil heap 113 to 115 m; the ground rising to 125 m by 500 m south | The ground of 2000, a mean over each cell of 30.9 by 21.5 m, good to several metres: that year's large buildings read 118 to 133 m north-west and south-east of the pad, and the pit 89 m at its deepest, half its depth |

Not found for the place: any photograph from the ground of the bunker's mound, the storage bunker,
the fence or the observation positions as they were in 1961; any Soviet plan of the site (the
general plan of "площадка 1"); the bunker's rooms. Searches in Russian for descriptions of the
bunker's outside returned nothing usable.

Not reached: epizodsspace's pages on the launch complex (its own host did not answer; two of its
texts were read through a mirror and the Internet Archive, above), TsKBTM's own drawings of the
launch system and its history at first hand, modela.lv's drawings of the 8U213 erector, the film
*Первый рейс к звёздам* (1961) itself, Golovanov's *Korolev*, and the 2011 collection of documents
(*Pervyi pilotiruemyi polet*). A drawing or a clear photograph of the erector standing at a Vostok
is what the pad's service hardware most wants: everything of its form is estimated.

Read again for what was fixed on the platform and in the pit (site-1.md, "The platform", "The flame
pit"), November 2026:

| Source | Where | Good for | Watch out for |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CIA, PIC/JR-2/61, **Figure 1** (plan and front elevation; p. 6 of the report, page 5 of the PDF) and Figure 2 (perspective), read at 500 dpi | (entry above) | **The plan of the platform and the pit base, dimensioned.** The five tracks' lines and ends on the platform, the two "gantry tracks", the small building, the north-east lip with the mast on it and the cut south-east corner, the fence; 115 ft over the pylons north to south; east of the platform the pit base (60 by 60 ft), the two trenches with a shelf outside each, 110 ft over all, and the sump in three compartments; in elevation the masts at 110 ft, the trenches below the pit base, the "possible base of flame deflector". Scaled at 4.73 px a foot | A diagram: dimensioned where it says so and only roughly to scale elsewhere (it draws the south-west mast 5 m from where its own text puts it). Many dimensions redacted: the gantry tracks' separation, the building's size, the trenches' depth |
| CIA, PIC/JR-2/60, the sections "Pit", "Launching Structure", "Underground Sump" and, of Launch Area "B", "Launching Structure" | (entry above) | The pit base 60 ft wide; the trenches 60 ft long and at least 60 ft apart, running to the sump's end compartments; the sump 75 ft wide, its face at least 10 ft above the trenches, its roof sloping up to the east; at the second pad in 1959 "a curved flame deflector plate" under construction that would not "protrude beyond the front" | The deflector at Launch Area "A" was never seen: it is under the platform |
| CIA, HTA/JR-4/58, "Pit", "Launching Structure", "Facilities on the Launching Platform" | (entry above) | The road into the pit "from the raised eastern terminus"; the four spurs on the platform and which is which; the "gantry-crane track"; the two towers, 110 ft, with "a large array on top at least 5 feet wide and 10 feet high", one on "a concrete lip which protrudes 10 feet" | Its "flame deflector plate 55 feet wide" is the pit base, as the 1960 report says |
| Zak, RussianSpaceWeb, *Baikonur's R-7 facilities: Site 1*: a photograph of a "scale model of the original R-7 pad in Tyuratam circa 1957"; a still of the site from the air in 1957; the flame trench in 2000 | [russianspaceweb.com/baikonur_r7_1.html](https://www.russianspaceweb.com/baikonur_r7_1.html) | **The flame deflector's form**: on the model a dark curved chute from the back wall under the opening down and out to the floor, between solid sides, with a niche and steel galleries above it; a short lattice with a box on it at each of two opposite corners of the deck. The text: the platform "a giant steel square 40 by 40 meters and three-story high" of sixteen railway bridge trusses, slid out onto hollow pillars | The model's photograph is 200 px wide and the museum is not named: a form, not a dimension. The trench in 2000 is paved in slabs throughout, which is later |
| Photographs of 12 April 1961 as carried by RussianSpaceWeb's page on the launch: the rocket leaving the pad, and the pilot walking to it | [russianspaceweb.com/vostok1_launch.html](https://www.russianspaceweb.com/vostok1_launch.html) | **One of the two masts**, beside the rocket at liftoff: a pale slender lattice, a wider head near its top, a thin rod above. **Rails lying flush in the concrete of the deck** in front of the walking party | Which mast it is depends on where the camera stood, which is not said. The liftoff picture's date is the page's attribution |
| The Soviet railway gauge | general reference | 1,524 mm (5 ft) until 1970, 1,520 mm since | Set down from general knowledge, not from a standard read here; that the range's track was laid to it is assumed |
| OpenStreetMap, the tracks and the pad's outline again, in the site's frame | (entry above) | The centre track 3.0 m north of the middle of the pad's outline; the other tracks 9.4 m and 13.8 m south and 14.6 m north of the centre track, the south track ending 13.6 m east of the axis and the short spur 1.7 m onto the abutment: within about a metre of the CIA's plan | The outline is a loose rectangle (47.8 by 42.5 m). World Imagery at 0.8 m a pixel could not say which of the two is out |

Read for the morning itself (site-1.md, "The morning's people and vehicles"; crew/ground-party.md), November 2026.
The film, Golovanov and the collection of documents, "not reached" above, were reached for this:

| Source | Where | Good for | Watch out for |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| «Первый рейс к звёздам» (ЦСДФ, 1961), colour, the morning's reel: 43:00 to 44:35 | YouTube, rAuM6bq5-bc, read frame by frame | **The pad on the morning, in colour.** 43:08 the bus on the concrete road (a LAZ-695B: the roof's air scoop; cream over blue; curtains). 43:26 from the erector's head as the bus stops: the bus a metre south of a track with another 4 m beyond, a dark saloon car, a dark box-bodied truck with white figures on its tail, some 25 people in groups, a low terracotta building with a flat roof, the concrete in slabs with the rails flush. 43:33 and 43:42 at eye height: tunics and peaked caps, no greatcoats, a general's red stripes, navy anoraks with red armbands, a white coat, a hand-held cine camera. 43:45 the party walking, from above. 44:05 the stair from the south, a dozen backs waving, two men on the wagon. 44:07 to 44:30 the lift: a ribbed car with two door leaves, a booth of plate, a gate of perforated plate | A compressed copy, its colour shifted. **Its shadows at 43:26 do not agree with 06:55** (site-1.md): they fit a Sun 20° further round. Some of this film's scenes were re-staged afterward; which, if any, of these is not known |
| Photographs of 12 April 1961, with where each camera stood worked out: before liftoff, the rocket venting, taken through the fence from the south (1,543 px); the liftoff in colour, from the same side; the cover of a booklet of 1961 lettered "12 IV 61" | as carried by several sites (RussianSpaceWeb; TASS; Kommersant); the booklet's cover is the only copy that reads the right way round | **The south-west mast is not there**; the north-east mast's head and lamps; the lip's bracket; the edge railing, two cabinets and a horn on the deck; a white building west of the pad (the propellant shed); the fence's pale pointed posts; the cable masts leaning to the left | **Mirrored copies circulate** (TASS 1208593 among them): do not measure from them. That the camera stood south is derived (site-1.md, "The platform") |
| A colour photograph of Gagarin with Korolev and Marshal Moskalenko at the pad | Wikimedia Commons, "Gagarin pered poletom" (3,780 px) | Korolev's dark overcoat and velour hat; the marshal's service tunic, boards and red-banded cap; behind them a terracotta wall with a frame of at least six floodlights on it | Where on the pad they stand is not said |
| A photograph of Gagarin waving from the stair's landing | Kommersant, KOG_053920 | The stair from the south, its stringers pierced; the boom's pierced girder; the lift's booth of plate with round ports; eleven backs at the stair's foot | |
| A colour photograph of the party walking to the stair | RussianSpaceWeb, the launch's page ("pad walk") | **A pale grey filler** behind the erector's wagon: panels, a low arched roof with ribs, a doorway at floor level with two men in it, a small window, «ОГНЕОПАСНО» and «НЕ СПУСК…» in black stencil, a number read as 64. On the wagon a red domed cover, a horn loudspeaker on a red-brown box, men at a rail. A doctor in white, a general, Korolev in his overcoat, a man in a grey jacket and flat cap with a red armband | Which filler the grey car is, and that the low green machine is the erector's wagon, are readings |
| Ya. Golovanov, «Королёв: факты и мифы», pp. 643 to 645 | read as text | The bus "white and blue", its thirteen aboard, the cameramen first out, the doctor who handed the pilot down; the State Commission "in a tight group" some ten metres off; Korolev's overcoat and "velour hat" | A biography written from interviews |
| «Первый пилотируемый полёт. Сборник документов» (2011), book 1: doc 104, the launch roster («боевой расчёт») of 10 April 1961, pp. 402 to 423; doc 85, the readiness report of March 1961, pp. 339 to 341 | read as a scan with its text | **Who was on the pad and when each left**: 651 and 37 inspectors, 381 of them soldiers; the readinesses (3 h 20, 1 h, 30 min, 15 min, 5 min) and where each crew went; a driver for "ГАЗ-63 №34-55-23"; the film crews. Doc 85: ten fire engines and five washing trucks for the March launch, the loudspeakers checked | It gives readinesses and destinations, not where anyone stood. "ПМЗ" is read as a fire engine |
| N. P. Kamanin, diary, 11 and 12 April 1961 | read as text | The bus at the pad at "8:50" | **By the range's clock, two hours ahead of Moscow's**: 06:50 Moscow time |
| V. Mikheev, memoir of the launch crew | proza.ru/2016/05/22/1305 | "It was nine in the morning, seven by Moscow time"; who "tore" the pilot from the crowd and walked him to the lift; the rail tractor drawing the erector and the fillers off | A memoir |
| CIA, NPIC/B-24/61, "Search for underground entrances, Launch Area of Launch Complex A" (9 September 1961), CIA-RDP78T04751A000200010027-4 | CIA reading room; Figure 1 read from the native 299 dpi scan | **The date of the U-2's second coverage: July 1959** (Figure 1's caption). A vertical photograph of the launch area with twelve numbered items: the buildings south of the embankment and outside the south fence, each described in the text from the photographs of August 1957 and July 1959 (what stood in both, what was added, what had gone): site1/south.py is built from it. The text gives the sizes of items 3 (20 by 15 ft), 6 (135 by 35 ft, an extension 40 by 20 ft) and 12 (325 by 130 ft), and the 35 ft between items 4 and 5. The spoil heap on the same print: a rounded white mass between two ditches, its west side scalloped (site1/rim.py) | A dithered half-tone: places good to ten metres. The other items' sizes are redacted, and are read off the print to a few metres: estimated. Nothing later shows this ground before April 1961 |

Also new: two buildings near the check-out shop, the double fence carried 1,300 ft north, a building 75 ft square 1.43 km north-west | **The photographs' date is redacted**, so only what another source dates to April 1961 is built from it. What stood on the pad is not described |
| The R-9's first launches: 9 April 1961 (failed) and 21 April 1961, from Site 51 | ru.wikipedia, «Р-9А»; A. Zak, RussianSpaceWeb, the R-9's page | That Site 51's pad stood on 12 April 1961, and that no rocket stood on it | General references, not documents |
| CIA, PIC/JR-2/60, p. 16, "Missile Assembly and Checkout Facility No 1"; OpenStreetMap way 173526300 | (entry above); openstreetmap.org | **The assembly and test building of Site 2**: 395 by 105 ft, 70 ft high, a shed 30 ft broad along its west side, three tracks through it; its place and the bearing of its long axis (028°) from today's map less the extension of 1973 to 1974 (places/tyuratam/site2/mik.py) | No photograph of it from 1957 to 1961 was found: its roof, windows, gates and colour are estimated |
| KH-7 mission 4018, 1 June 1965, frame 11 (DZB00401800073H011001), 14,310 by 14,504 px | Wikimedia Commons, category "PD US NRO" (from SpaceFrom.Space) | Sought as a third view of Site 1's cable masts. **Site 1 is not on it**: a rail yard, a barracks grid and small pads of another part of the range | Its page gives the range's coordinates, not the frame's. The KH-4A frames of 1965 beside it are at some 2.7 m a pixel: too coarse for a mast |

Only the bus's type is the film's |
| The vehicles' and the locomotive's engines at Site 1 (place.ts `engines`) | ru.wikipedia, «ГАЗ-21» (Силовой агрегат), «ЛАЗ-695», «ЗИЛ-158» (Технические характеристики), «ГАЗ-63», «ГАЗ-51», «ТЭМ1», «ТЭМ2», «ТЭ1» (Дизель; Органы управления); Za Rulyom, «ЛАЗ-695Е: конструкция» (2025), [zr.ru](https://www.zr.ru/content/articles/973005-laz695ekonstrukciya/) | The ZMZ-21A four at 4,000 rpm; the ZIL-158's 5.55 litre six (also called the ZIL-124) at 2,800, the LAZ-695B's; the GAZ-51 six at 2,800; the TEM1's 2D50 at 740 rpm and the D50's lowest position, 270; the gearboxes: the GAZ-21's three-speed manual (3.12, 1.77, 1.00; reverse 3.74) and the GAZ-63's four-speed (6.4, 3.09, 1.69, 1.00; reverse 7.82), from their infoboxes' Трансмиссия | Reference works, not the makers' literature. The LAZ-695B's gearbox is not carried: the ratios the reference gives are the 695E's ZIL-130 five-speed, and the early 695Bs had an overdrive fifth. No idle speed found for any road vehicle. The locomotive's type is not recorded: the TEM1's figures are taken (`assumed`) |

### The steppe at Tyuratam

Read for the ground and what grows round Site 1 (missions/ussr/places/tyuratam/steppe.ts; site-1.md, "The ground and what grows"), November 2026.
Most dated photographs of "Baikonur" plants were taken on the left bank at the river crossing, 30 km south of the pad;
only Lednev's and the 1930 specimen are from the right bank, where the pad is.

| Source | Where | Good for | Watch out for |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Veselova P.V. and others, "Антропофильный элемент флоры пустынной части долины р. Сырдарья", *Trudy Inst. Botaniki i Fitointrodukcii* 23(7), Almaty 2017, pp. 3-4 | botsad.kz | The Syr Darya's desert valley: grey-brown and light-brown soils, takyrs and solonchaks "widespread", small sand massifs; the channel 120 to 240 m with levees to 5 m, the floodplain to 15 km, old channels | Regional, not of the pad |
| Chertok B., *Rockets and People*, vol. II (NASA SP-2006-4110), pp. 314, 335, 357; vol. III (NASA SP-2009-4110), ch. 3, p. 57 | NASA History Office | The clay crust ("steppe asphalt") and tulips on it; trucks grinding it to dust, the dusty roads "two to three kilometers wide" (1955 to 1957, from the builder Gurovich); "the almost green steppe of springtime" with tulips (April 1957); the steppe "burst into bloom with the stubby tulips earlier than usual" in April 1961; burnt "reddish-gray" by summer | A memoir written decades later |
| Lednev, observations in Kyzylorda oblast, 3 August 2017 and 26 April 2018 | plantarium.ru | On the cosmodrome's own land: *Artemisia terrae-albae* with *Krascheninnikovia ceratoides*; *Anabasis salsa* with *Carex physodes*; wormwood with *Ceratocarpus*, *Carex subphysodes*, *Bassia prostrata*; *Salsola incanescens* in hollows; wormwood-sedge on sand; *Tulipa borszczowii* in flower | Photographs with notes, not a survey: no cover figures |
| Danilevsky, observations round Baikonur, April 2009 to 2011 | plantarium.ru | A takyr and solonchaks in closed hollows; the floodplain's *Salicornia*, reeds, tamarisk, *Halostachys*, *Nitraria*; *Haloxylon aphyllum*, *Calligonum*, *Alhagi* on the left bank; early April flowering (*Gagea*, *Ceratocephala*, *Chorispora*, *Iris tenuifolia*) | The left bank: what is not on the right bank too is not drawn |
| Dimeeva L.A., Salmukhanbetova Zh., *Eurasian J. | Jin and others, *Phytotaxa* 686(1): 48-50, 2025 | | *Artemisia terrae-albae* 8 to 30 cm, grey-white felted | Of the species, not the place |

Dombrovsky, 25 April 1930); yellow through orange-red to scarlet with a dark base; about 30 cm, half of it under ground; late April to early May; in wormwood-biyurgun communities | How many to a square metre: not found |
| Kubentayev and others, *PhytoKeys* 250: 95-163, 2024 | | The tulips of Kazakhstan: *T. borszczowii* not endemic (Uzbekistan too); *T. buhseana* flowering March to April | |
| Kazaly (Kazalinsk) climate, from Pogoda.ru.net, as carried by en.wikipedia | | April mean 12.5 °C, March 2.0 °C: the snow gone by early April (derived) | |

The sandy unit is a soil, not bare sand: not drawn |

What a pale area was (ground worked over, or graded) is a reading; the station road is plain on the 1962 frame and faint on the 1960 one; nothing on the 1962 frame alone is drawn |

| Chertok, *Rockets and People*, vol. Kazalinsk is 96 km west of Site 1, Kara-Kum 147 km north-east |
| *Flora of the USSR* (English edition), vol. III (*Carex physodes*), vol. VI (*Anabasis salsa*, *Salsola*, *Eurotia ceratoides*), vol. XXVI (*Artemisia terrae-albae*, species 135) | [archive.org](https://archive.org/download/floraofussr26bota/) | What each plant is in autumn: the wormwood flowering in August, white when young and later greyish-green; the biyurgun's fruit red, July to August; the saltworts' wings purple, rose or yellowish turning brown; the teresken's hairs "finally rufescent" | Of the species, not the place; no colour photograph of the cosmodrome in October or March has been read |

### The launch vehicle (8K72K)

Read for [vostok-8k72k.md](../missions/ussr/vostok/vehicles/vostok-8k72k.md). **No OKB-1 or
TsSKB-Progress drawing or table of the launcher was found**: what follows is print and web
secondary sources, one schematic drawing and photographs. Each page's text was downloaded and read
unless it says otherwise.

| Source | Where | Good for | Watch out for |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| V. Minakov (engineer), «Ракета, поднявшая в космос Ю. Гагарина», *Modelist-Konstruktor* (the magazine's own site, posted 12 April 2017; the issue it first ran in is not stated there) | [modelist-konstruktor.com](https://modelist-konstruktor.com/aviacziya/raketa-podnyavshaya-v-kosmos-yu-gagarina) | **The best single source found.** A table of the 8K72: 38.36 m, 10.3 m over the rudders, 287 t, dry 29 t; strap-on 19.2 m without nozzles, 2.68 m, 3.75 t dry; core with the lattice 28.75 m, 2.95 m; third stage with fairing 9.61 m, Block E 2.58 m, fairing 2.7 m. In the text: the lattice 1.26 m, Block E for Vostok 2.98 m with its lengthened adapter, the fairing 6.6 m with its opening, Block E's ring tanks and steering nozzles, 7,984 and 1,472 kg, the tank layout of both blocks, the tip balls, the ties and dovetails at the foot, how the boosters leave, the polished steel shields | Its drawings (a dimensioned general arrangement with the kinds of seam marked) are captioned on the page and **not shown**: the scan of the issue is wanted. Its table's thrusts are round (4 × 82 tf) |
| Semenov (ed.), *Raketno-kosmicheskaya korporatsiya "Energiya" imeni S. P. Koroleva, 1946–1996*, the chapters on the R-7, the 8K72 and Vostok | epizodsspace.airbase.ru/bibl/energia46-96/ (02 and 03.html), through the Internet Archive's copy of March 2021 | RKK Energia's own account: all five blocks light on the ground, 32 chambers each with its igniter, steering chambers of 2.5 tf by Melnikov's group (two on a booster, four on the core), tanks load-bearing with the oxidiser forward, 287 t for this flight, Block E of 8 t steered by nozzles on the turbine's exhaust, hot staging | **No stage dimension is in it.** Its figures (the R-7's layout, Block E with the spacecraft) were not retrieved |
| Brügge, *Space Launch Vehicles*: Vostok design, propulsion and engine history tables | [b14643.eu, Semyorka/Design/Vostok-1](https://b14643.eu/Spacerockets_1/East_Europe_1/Semyorka/Design/Vostok-1.htm), [Propulsion/history](https://b14643.eu/Spacerockets_1/East_Europe_1/Semyorka/Propulsion/history.htm) | NPO Energomash's published table of the RD-107 and RD-108 by vehicle, reproduced: for the 8K72K, 815.9 and 997.7 kN, 2,510 and 3,069 N·s/kg; 744.3 and 945.4 kN, 2,432 and 3,089. Stage masses and propellant loads, the core's three diameters (2.05, 2.95, 2.57 m), the fairing as 6.74 m by 2.58 | A compiler's tables: sources are named for the page (Varfolomeyev in *Spaceflight*, Energomash), not for each figure. Burn times are computed, not reported. Disagrees with Minakov on Block E (2.47 m across, 2.86 m long) |
| Wade, Encyclopedia Astronautica: Vostok 8K72K stage data; RD-0109 | [astronautix.com/v/vostok8k72k.html](http://www.astronautix.com/v/vostok8k72k.html) | Stage masses (43.3 and 3.71 t; 100.4 and 6.8 t; 7,775 and 1,440 kg), the RD-0109's 121 kg and 50 bar | Gives the vehicle's height as 30.84 m and the core's diameter as 2.99 |
| Zak, RussianSpaceWeb: the Vostok launcher; the launch of 12 April 1961 | [vostok_lv.html](https://www.russianspaceweb.com/vostok_lv.html), [vostok1_launch.html](https://www.russianspaceweb.com/vostok1_launch.html) | Engine No. 17, the fairing's 2.7 m and 0.8 t and its opening, dry masses; the ascent as Gagarin felt it, 119, 154 and 299 s, the core running half a second long; colour film stills of Vostok launches (the paint and the frost) | Says the film shown as Gagarin's liftoff is of 28 July 1960 |
| Wonus, "The Case of the SS-6", *Studies in Intelligence* (CIA, released 1994) | [cia.gov, The-Case-of-SS6.pdf](https://www.cia.gov/resources/csi/static/The-Case-of-SS6.pdf) | What the vehicle shown at Paris in 1967 told the analysts: the core's lower tank plainly narrower than its upper, tanks that are not cylinders, propellant volumes in the ratio 1.6 to 1, one engine design on both stages | **It contains no dimension.** The measured figures the CIA must hold were not found in the reading room |
| Portree, *Mir Hardware Heritage*, NASA RP-1357, the outline drawing of the Vostok launcher | [Wikimedia Commons, *Vostok rocket drawing*](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Vostok_rocket_drawing.png) | The one side elevation measured: proportions of fairing, Block E, lattice, core and boosters, the hatch opening's height | A schematic. Its boosters are 19.1 m tall with a 3.3 m cylinder, which would not hold their propellant; its core is 5 % too wide |
| Photographs of the vehicle at the Tsiolkovsky museum, Kaluga | Commons: [*Kaluga - Tsiolkovsky Park and Museum (08)*](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Kaluga_-_Tsiolkovsky_Park_and_Museum_(08).jpg), [*Wikiexpedition to Kaluga (by Dmitry Rozhkov) 134*](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Wikiexpedition_to_Kaluga_(by_Dmitry_Rozhkov)_134.jpg), [*Ракета-носитель «Восток»*](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Ракета-носитель_«Восток».jpg) | The outside as hardware: the boosters' cone and cylinder, bands and scalloped doublers, cable ducts, tip fairings, the lattice, the rudders. The second shows it lying on its transporter, side on | Russian Wikipedia calls it the flight's spare («дублирующая копия (не макет)»), citing a tourist site. Museum paint and lettering; its fairing's nose is curved |
| Photograph of the article at VDNKh, Moscow, from directly below | Commons: [*Moscow, VDNKh, engine arrangement on the R-7 rocket*](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Moscow,_VDNKh,_engine_arrangement_on_the_R-7_rocket_(10656172226).jpg) | Where all 32 chambers are: the main chambers' square, the steering chambers either side of each rudder and at the core's corners, the tie struts, the heat shields | A display article of a later build, painted, with red covers in its nozzles |
| Cutaway of Block E in its Luna form (RIA Novosti, Mokletsov) | Commons: [*Block-E rocket stage*](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Block-E_rocket_stage.png) | The two ring tanks, the engine in the hole, steering nozzles at the foot, the lattice's V struts and the deflector on the core | The Luna stage, with a short adapter and no spacecraft frame |
| «Восток (ракета-носитель)», Russian Wikipedia, carrying the Soviet encyclopedia's text | [ru.wikipedia.org](https://ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/Восток_(ракета-носитель)) | 38.36 m, 10.3 m, 287 t; blocks 28.75 by 2.95 m and 19.8 by 2.68 m; the steering nozzles on turbine exhaust | Uncited in the article; reads as *Kosmonavtika: Entsiklopediya* (1985), not seen |

### The mission as flown

Read for [mission.md](../missions/ussr/vostok/flights/vostok-1/mission.md) and the flight's
`orbital.ts`, 12 November 2026. Each download was given 60 seconds and two tries.

| Source | Where | Good for | Watch out for |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| The OKB-1 report, Gagarin's report and the questions (entries above), read again in full for the flight's instants | Wilson Center records 260547, 260544 and the questions | The braking engine's sequence to a tenth of a second (command, "launch" 2.2 s on, at its mode 1.5 s after, fuel pressure gone at 42.2 s, the timer at 44.0 s); 6 deg/s in pitch; the radio channels; the reserve canopy; "TDU directed to the Sun"; what the Vzor showed; the loads by feel | The archive's site now answers a plain download with 403; the copies read were fetched on 11 November. The translation's "IKN" gloss is doubtful |
| Zak, RussianSpaceWeb: "Gagarin orbits the Earth aboard Vostok" and "Vostok lands successfully" | [vostok1_orbit.html](https://www.russianspaceweb.com/vostok1_orbit.html), [vostok1_landing.html](https://www.russianspaceweb.com/vostok1_landing.html) | The Zarya-3 and Vesna exchanges with their minutes; the planned and flown timeline; the table of orbits (planned 180 by 230 km; TASS; US radar of Block E, 190.6 by 326.2 km); 132 of 136 m/s; Lissov's 150 to 160 km at 10:36; the planned landing near Pestravka (52.40° N 49.96° E); the two accounts of the core's late cutoff; the wind and the Volga. Read in full | Its table of the report's orbit has slips (372 km, "64.57"), and its landing coordinates print minutes as decimals. Lissov's work is at second hand here |
| Siddiqi, The Space Review, 2015 (entry above), read again | | The Granit-5V's 67 minutes; the valve on the RD-0109; ten days of supplies against thirty of decay; 10 to 12 g | Puts the short landing to the lost second of burn, which the coast shows to have the wrong sign |
| Ustinov and others, 30 March 1961 (entry above) | | Decay "over two to seven days" as the fallback; landing between 65° north and south | |
| Vallado, *Fundamentals of Astrodynamics and Applications*, 4th ed., examples 2-2, 2-5, 2-6 | the book; figures set down from it | What `src/world/orbit.ts` is held to: Kepler's equation, elements from a state and back | **Quoted from memory, not from the open book**: the three examples' figures agree with the code to their last printed digit, which a misremembered set would not, but an edition and page are not recorded |
| Wikipedia, "Vostok 1" | [en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vostok_1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vostok_1) | The monument's coordinates (51.270682° N 45.99727° E, from a map link) | Tertiary. Prints "about 8 g" without a source, and the period as 89.1 min beside the report's heights |

Could not be reached, or not tried in this pass: NASA NSSDC's record 1961-012A (an error page on
both tries); Chertok, *Rockets and People*, vol. 3 (NASA SP-4110), RKK Energia's history for the
flight, the FAI's record file and Encyclopedia Astronautica's Vostok 1 page (not tried: the time went
to the primary reports); *Pervyi pilotiruemyi polet* (2011) and Lissov's articles in *Novosti
Kosmonavtiki* (not online where looked for).

### The ascent and the descent

Read for the ascent flown (src/sim/staged.ts; the flight's `orbital.ts`, `ascent` and `descent`; vostok-8k72k.md, "What
a staged trajectory will need"), 13 November 2026. Each text was downloaded and read unless it says otherwise.

| Source | Where | Good for | Watch out for |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Gagarin's flight task, page 1 («Полетное задание для полета испытателя-космонавта ... на изделии 8К72 № Е103-16 с объектом "ЗКА" № 3»), ЦА РКВ ф. 19, оп. 114сс, д. 15, as pictured in Tsygikalo, «Предварительная… Промежуточная… Главная… Поехали!», *Kommersant Nauka* No. 9, 14 April 2021 | [kommersant.ru/doc/4769617](https://www.kommersant.ru/doc/4769617) | **Primary**: the launch at 09:07 ± 7 min; 1 h 46 min, 1.03 orbits; the aiming azimuth 34°37′59″.2; the planned landing 52.5° N 47.7° E; the B-137M's charging times, 308.29 s ("Разделение") and 399.88 s ("Наддув"); the third stage's main cutoff at 2,283.52 m/s of apparent velocity. Read off the 1,200-pixel image | A photograph of one page: the third numeral of 2,283.52 could be a 6, and the last figures of the seconds are uncertain |
| Arianespace, *Soyuz from the Guiana Space Centre User's Manual*, issue 2, March 2012, Fig. 1.5.1a "LV property data" | arianespace.com (the PDF) | **What Brügge's R-7 block masses are**: Soyuz-ST's (a strap-on 44,413 kg gross, 3,784 dry, 27,900 kg of oxygen and 11,260 of kerosene; the core 99,765 and 6,545, 63,800 and 26,300), the peroxide and nitrogen outside them. Read as text | **A different vehicle**, forty years later: not an input |
| Gagarin's flight task, all three sheets and the cover letter (ЦА РКВ ф. 19, оп. 114сс, д. 15, лл. 125–128), Ministry of Defence, «Покорители космоса», 2021 | 60cosmonauts.mil.ru (Wayback captures of 12 April 2021 and 10 September 2022; the scans on static.tildacdn.com) | **Primary**: sheet 126 as above, 2,283.52 m/s settled on the 800-pixel scan; sheet 127: the TDU's integrator at 136 m/s, pitch programme No. 20, the regulators' settings (17.36 kg/s through Block E's pump); sheet 128: the mixture ratios, the B-256KA at 159.5 s, the signatures. No core cutoff setting | The radio system's own task and the «Основные данные по изделию 8К72 № Е10316» (the cover letter's second enclosure) are not published |
| Lissov and Afanasyev, «106 минут Гагарина в свете рассекреченных документов», *Novosti Kosmonavtiki* 2011 No. 6, pp. 2–11 | Roscosmos's PDF, nk2011-06-.pdf (Wayback capture of 7 September 2022) | Written page by page from «Первый пилотируемый полёт» (2011): the launch cyclogram (book 1, p. 431: «Наддув» 304.74 s, «Предварительная» 305.74, «Главная» 312.74, Block E's main command 674.68); NIIP-5's report (book 2, pp. 54–69): «Наддув» from the integrator at 305.20 s, the third stage lit at 321.42 s and cut at 677.08 s at 2,285.55 m/s, the core 22.0 m/s over; masses (291,619 kg at liftoff, Block E 1,210 kg dry and 6,576 kg filled, the fairing 582 kg) | Secondary; the masses' page in the documents is not given |
| «Первый пилотируемый полёт. Сборник документов», books 1 and 2 (Rodina Media, 2011) | not reached | The documents themselves; read here only through Novosti Kosmonavtiki and Gammal and Tuchin's preprint (and, for the cabin, as printed in the transcript and Gagarin's report) | Known of, not read whole |
| Tsygikalo, *Kommersant Nauka* (above), the text | (above) | Gagarin's report quoted on the stages of thrust and the weightlessness before the third stage | A magazine's account |
| Gammal and Tuchin, «Реконструкция траектории полёта КА "Восток-1"», Keldysh Institute of Applied Mathematics, preprint 2025 No. 15 (CC BY 4.0) | [keldysh.ru/papers/2025/prep2025_15.pdf](https://keldysh.ru/papers/2025/prep2025_15.pdf) | **«Первый пилотируемый полёт» (2011) quoted by page**: the strap-ons separated on the integrator's command at 119.50 s, done 0.03 s later; the fairing at 09:09:34.3; the spacecraft at 09:18:26.8; no instant for the core's cutoff in the telemetry (p. 60); NII-4's orbit "from the gyro platform's data and angle measurements", its time of osculation not stated (p. 42); landing 51°16′14″ N 45°59′50″ E, 43 m | Its own ascent is a Soyuz's from Guiana turned to Tyuratam, and its orbit is fitted to the landing: neither is used |
| Baturin, «Подлинная история полета Гагарина», *Novaya Gazeta*, 12 April 2021 | [novayagazeta.ru](https://novayagazeta.ru/articles/2021/04/11/podlinnaia-istoriia-poleta-gagarina) | Why the apogee was 327 km: the radio guidance's power at 156 s, the core 0.46 s late, 22.0 m/s; Block E 2.4 s long; 25.43 m/s in all. The braking engine's normal running ended at 40.1 s, "an overshoot of 600 km". The descent: radar at 10:48, ejection at 10:49, the main 50 s later at 4 km, the reserve at 3 km; 12 g | An editor of the 2011 documents writing for a newspaper, with no footnotes. Its descent clock disagrees with R and Z |
| Chertok, *Rockets and People*, vol. 2 (SP-2006-4110) pp. 301-305, 386, 443; vol. 3 (SP-2009-4110) pp. 73, 80-81, 291 | (entries above) | The launch sequence's stages of thrust; the three integrators voting two of three; the RKS and SOB regulators; Vostok 1's radio command failing and the integrator's cutoff; the braking engine's check valve and 132 for 136 m/s | A memoir. Vol. 3 p. 73's "0.25 meters per second" is, its editor notes, probably 25 |
| в/ч 11284, reports of 17 October and 14 November 1957 on the launch of 8K71PS No. M1-1 (Sputnik), TsAMO f. 19, op. 110ss, d. 14 | [rvsn.info, doc 1028](https://rvsn.info/library/docs/doc_1_1028.html), [doc 1029](https://rvsn.info/library/docs/doc_1_1029.html) | **The same first two stages flown and measured**: the start's stages of thrust; the strap-ons to their first intermediate stage at 109.94 s, separated by the integrator at 116.38 s (57.7 km up, 54.8 km down range, 2,189 m/s); the core's cutoff at 295.6 s and 7,733 m/s; where the strap-ons fell (617 to 632 km); each engine's measured flow and impulse | **A different vehicle**: two stages, no Block E, a lighter payload. A comparison, never an input |
| Starsem, *Soyuz User's Manual*, ST-GTD-SUM-01, issue 3, April 2001 | starsem.com (the PDF) | The Soyuz's ascent from Baikonur: pitch against time (Fig. 2-4, read off the drawing), height and speed against time, 34.8° of azimuth for 64.9°; the start's stages of thrust; Block I lit 2 s before the core's cutoff | **A different vehicle** on the same first two stages, with a far heavier upper stage. Used for the vertical rise's length only, labelled |
| lpre.de, «РД-107 и РД-108», a compilation citing Gakhun et al., *Конструкция и проектирование ЖРД* (1989) and others | [lpre.de/energomash/RD-107](http://www.lpre.de/energomash/RD-107/index.htm) | The chambers' exits (720 mm main, 310 mm steering) and throats; the RD-107's turbopump (226 kg/s of oxygen, 91.4 of kerosene, 8.8 of peroxide, 1.75 of nitrogen: so its published impulse is reckoned on the first three); the start and shutdown sequence; the RD-108's final stage on its steering chambers (10 s on the 8D75); that the 8K72K flew the 8D74 and 8D75; the turbine's spent steam «выбрасывается через выхлопные патрубки со скоростью 450 м/с, создавая тягу 7 кН», 0.14 MPa at the turbine's outlet (the pipes' number and places are not given: the model's two are estimated) | A compilation: its cyclogram is not sourced and reads as the ICBM's |
| KBKhA, «РД0109» | [kbkha.ru](https://kbkha.ru/deyatel-nost/raketnye-dvigateli-dlya-kosmicheskoy-otrasli/raketnye-dvigateli-ao-kbha/rd0109/) | The maker's page: 5.56 tf in vacuum, 323.5 s, engine No. 17 flew Gagarin | No nozzle size, no burn time |
| Bobkov (OKB-1), «Космический корабль "Восток"», *Krylya Rodiny* 1991 No. 4 and 6 | testpilot.ru/espace/bibl/k-r/1991/4-5vostok.html | **The parachutes**: the module's 1.5, 18 and 574 m² at 7, 4 and 2.5 km, landing at 10 m/s; the pilot's 2, 83.5 and 56 m², the reserve put out automatically on this flight only, 6 m/s; the hatch at 7 km and 220 m/s and the seat 2 s later | A designer's article without footnotes. "3 s" and "4 km" for the main canopy cannot both hold |
| Gagarin Cosmonaut Training Centre, «Корабль "Восток"» | [gctc.ru](https://www.gctc.ru/main.php?id=296) | The same parachute figures; the pilot down at 5 m/s | Unsigned |

Not found anywhere tried: the 8K72K's pitch programme, a drag coefficient for any R-7, the levels of the engines'
intermediate and final stages of thrust, the launch azimuth as flown, where Vostok 1's strap-ons and core fell, and the
day's winds aloft at Tyuratam or Saratov. «Первый пилотируемый полёт» itself and Lissov and Afanasyev, «106 минут
Гагарина в свете рассекреченных документов», *Novosti Kosmonavtiki* 2011 No. 6 (roscosmos.ru answered 403) are where
they would be looked for next.

Read for what a hand's staging does (sim/staging.ts; vostok-1/orbital.ts `hot`, `start`, `limits`, `aborts`, November 2026):

| Source | Where | What it gave | What it is not |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Molodtsov (OKB-1), «История проектирования корабля "Восток"», *Космический альманах* No. 5, 2001 | epizodsspace.narod.ru/bibl/akiem/5v-vostok.html | Vostok's ascent abort by time: the seat to 40 s, the engines shut and the seat at 7 km to 150 s, the gyros' limit contacts and the descent module's separation to 700 s, the whole ship to 730 s | Secondary, by an OKB-1 author; flown on the orbital deck's abort (X twice: vostok-8k72k.ts `aborts`, `escape`) |
| Volkhonsky, lecture notes on launcher design (Moscow, 2012) | studfile.net/preview/8823953/page:26/ | That the heat shield on the stage below protects its top and turns the jet across the flight | Not of the R-7 by name; no figure |
| Zak, RussianSpaceWeb, "Stage II of Soyuz rocket" and "Soyuz 18-1" | russianspaceweb.com/soyuz_lv_stage2.html, soyuz18-1.html | Soyuz's titanium-covered deflector cone over the instrument bay on the oxidiser tank; Soyuz 18a joined about 2 s under its third stage's thrust before the latches broke, nothing burst | Soyuz, not the 8K72K; secondary |
| Terekhov, «32 березовые палки…», Habr, 4 April 2016 | habr.com | The R-7's igniters: two pyrotechnic charges on a T-shaped wooden stand in each chamber, single-use | Secondary; that nothing could relight them in flight is inferred |
| Shakmaev et al., *Инженерный журнал* 2024 No. 3, DOI 10.18698/2308-6033-2024-3-2345 | the journal | Soyuz-2's greatest dynamic pressure (34 to 45 kPa) and its 3-degree tolerance off the wind there | Soyuz-2's tolerance, not a strength, and not the R-7's |
| Arianespace, Soyuz CSG User's Manual, issue 2, 2012, §3.2.1, §3.4.3.2, §A5.1.4 | as above | Soyuz-2's longitudinal load (under 4.3 g), its fairing let go under 1,135 W/m², its third stage lit through the truss before separation | Forty years later |

Not found: how long the 8K72K's deflector was made to take Block E's jet, any structural limit of the R-7 (q alpha, axial
load, the fairing's dynamic pressure), and how the RD-0109 was started (its starter, its igniter, its number of starts).

### Vostok 2

Read for [Vostok 2](../missions/ussr/vostok/flights/vostok-2/README.md), 13 November 2026. Each
page's text was downloaded and read unless it says otherwise.

| Source | Where | Good for | Watch out for |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| *Pravda* (7 August 1961, extra edition, and 12 August), *Izvestiya* (8 August) and *Komsomolskaya Pravda* (8 August), translated by J. L. Zygielbaum as "The Flight of Vostok II", JPL Astronautics Information Translation No. 24, 2 October 1961 (NASA N65-83201) | [archive.org, nasa_techdoc_19650070800](https://archive.org/details/nasa_techdoc_19650070800), its OCR text | **The Soviet record of the days themselves.** TASS orbit by orbit (09:00, 178 by 257 km, 64°56′, 88.6 min, 4,731 kg, Signal on 19.995 MHz, the meals, 18:15, 02:37, 25 h 18 min); the morning at the pad (the orange suit, "a helmet with the inscription USSR", the log book with its pencil on a string); the press conference of 11 August: Keldysh, Titov (manual control at 10:00 and on the seventh orbit, the Konvas, the ejection admitted, 10:18), Yazdovsky (10 to 22 °C, oxygen 25 to 27 %, humidity 55 to 75 %, ten days' supplies, the vestibular trouble in one sentence), Kotelnikov (15.765, 20.006 and 143.625 MHz; two television systems, 100 lines and the new 400, ten frames a second, each with its own transmitter; the broadcast receiver; the tape recorder). Read in full | A translation, and OCR of a typescript: its figures' captions survive, the figures do not. Keldysh's "24 hours and 18 minutes" is a slip. What was censored is absent: the sickness beyond a sentence, the failed separation, that Gagarin had ejected too |
| The same reports in Russian: «700 000 километров в космосе» (1961), the collection of the newspapers' texts | testpilot.ru/espace/bibl/titov-g/700000-iz/ (a mirror of epizodsspace), parts 01 and 02 | The wording: «На нем оранжевого цвета скафандр, шлем с надписью: «СССР»»; «оптическим прибором 3- и 5-кратного увеличения»; «4.731 килограмм». Read by search, not end to end | The mirror's later parts are stubs |
| Titov's report in the FAI record file («Дело о рекордах…»), pages 11 and 12, signed | as two page images in Terekhov's article (below) | Liftoff "6 часов 00 минут по гринвичскому времени"; manual control at 10.00 Moscow time in the shadow; the broadcast receiver; lunch about 12:30; woke at the start of the 13th orbit; "система приземления с отделением кресла"; 10 часов 18 минут. Read as images | The public version: it says the same as the press conference |
| «Первый пилотируемый полёт» (2011), book 2: doc. 175, OKB-1's report on the changes to «Восток-3А» No. 4, no later than 3 July 1961, signed by Tikhonravov and Feoktistov (pp. 136 to 137; ЦА РКК «Энергия», арх. 2238, л. 173–176); doc. 184 (pp. 152 to 153), doc. 187, the flight programme (pp. 157 to 159), doc. 188 (pp. 167 to 168) | epizodsspace.airbase.ru/bibl/pervyi-pilotiruemyi/pp_book2.pdf (Internet Archive copy of 17 November 2017), read as its text, November 2026 | **The list of No. 4's changes**, item by item: a third bottle in the manual orientation system; the Konvas with 300 m of colour film; «оптическая система «Визир» с 3-х и 5-ти кратным увеличением и углами зрения 24° и 15°»; VHF on at the pilot's wish; receiver and alarm to loudspeaker or headphones; a handier log book; remote control of hatch No. 1's porthole shutter; the air conditioning replaced; the TDU's fixes; the improved manual orientation unit BRU-1A; one half-set of the 100-line Signal–Tral-T replaced by the 400-line Topaz–Yastreb; the food heater, the APO and the AFA-39 taken out. Doc. 187: the pilot's picture by Tral-T–Seliger and Topaz–Yastreb, the cameras switched on in turn | Nothing in it of the board, the Globus, the panel or the lamps, or where the Konvas and the Vizir were fixed; what the BRU-1A was is not said. The food heater was taken out, not put in |
| Chertok, *Rockets and People*, vol. 3 (NASA SP-2009-4110), ch. 7, pp. 187 to 194, and p. 494 | [nasa.gov, vol. 3](https://www.nasa.gov/pdf/636007main_RocketsPeopleVolume3-ebook.pdf); read as text for these pages | The modifications in his own words (the list by Bushuyev and Feoktistov, "at least a week", the radio, the "more respectable" television with Rosselevich, Signal); 3 a.m., 4 a.m., 5 a.m. on the morning; 0900; the orbit confirmed at 0920; the control post through the night; **the cause of the failed separation** (the pyro knife cut the cable carrying the plate's jettison command, a cable run wrongly; a repeat of the fourth and fifth ship-satellites); the descent module lost in the Voskhod drop test at Feodosiya | A memoir. No figure of the ship or the orbit |
| Siddiqi, *Challenge to Apollo* (entry under "Read"), pp. 290 to 295, 412, the launch log and Table IB | the same PDF, read as text extracted from it | **The serial numbers**: Vostok 2, 6 August 1961, 0900, 11F63, 3KA No. 4, 8K72K, E103-17. The objectives signed on 7 July; the leak in the core; Zritel, the Konvas and its broken exposure meter; "of the two cameras, one had a resolution of 400 lines"; the new regeneration system; 10 °C and the two fans (from Kamanin); Table IB: 4,731 kg, 17 orbits, 183 by 244 km, 64.93°, 01:01:18 | Its text and its table disagree twice: 178 by 257 km against 183 by 244, and "one day, one hour, and eleven minutes" against 25 h 18. Has the modules held by "several straps" (from Golovanov), which Chertok and the 2011 documents correct |
| Zak, RussianSpaceWeb, "Vostok-2 mission: First day in space" | [russianspaceweb.com/vostok2.html](https://www.russianspaceweb.com/vostok2.html) | **Titov's report to the State Commission of 8 August 1961 at second hand**, minute by minute: 08:59:57; the lock keyed at about 10:00; the nitrogen's pressures (160, 150, 135, 120, 110, 102); the filming at the start of the second orbit; the Globus out of reach; the sickness; 17:30 to 17:55; the braking burn at about 09:57 for 40 s; the lamps that stayed lit; 10:07; the ejection, the reserve canopy, the train; the capsule five kilometres off. The upgraded air conditioning and its 12 days of tests. Film stills of the morning (the helmet lettered СССР at the bus and at the hatch) and of the television picture. Read in full | Its table gives the landing as 10:11 and the text gives Titov's landing as 10:11 too, beside 25 h 18 min; "18 orbits". The liftoff second names no document. Its stills are 200 pixels wide |
| Pervushin, «Тяжёлый рейс», warspot.ru, 2021 | [warspot.ru/20176-tyazhyolyy-reys](https://warspot.ru/20176-tyazhyolyy-reys) (now carried under another byline) | The same report quoted in Russian (the tube of juice, «муторное», the fan and 10 degrees, the hatch and the nose, the reserve parachute); «3КА №4», «8К72К № Е103-17»; the burn of 45 s; the pea field 13 km from Krasny Kut. Read by search across the whole text | A popular article; its sources are listed, not cited line by line |
| Kamanin, *Skrytyi kosmos*, the diary for 1961 | [militera.lib.ru/db/kamanin_np/61.html](http://militera.lib.ru/db/kamanin_np/61.html) | **The lock's code, 145**, which he checked on the ship at 08:00 on 12 April («проверил шифр (145) логического замка»: read in a text copy of book 1, November 2026; Siddiqi and Gallay's memoir have 125: Vostok 1's mission.md, "The lock's code"). The air drier's failed trials before Vostok 1 (13-day tests of Voronin's new driers, lithium chloride against charcoal, the old one flown); that he and Gagarin were abroad from 27 April to 7 August, so there is no entry for the flight; December 1961: the cabin at 10 degrees because both fans were on by mistake. Read by search | No entry for the preparation or the flight itself |
| Terekhov (lozga), «Забытый полет «Орла»», Habr, 2016 | [habr.com/ru/articles/396713](https://habr.com/ru/articles/396713/) | The photograph of the Konvas shown as Titov's; the two pages of the record file. Read in full | A blog. Its account is from Chertok |
| "The First Photographer in Space", *Air & Space* (Smithsonian), 5 August 2011 | [smithsonianmag.com](https://www.smithsonianmag.com/air-space-magazine/the-first-photographer-in-space-44654847/) | Titov holding a Konvas (FotoSoyuz); "Gagarin, incredibly, didn't carry a camera". Read in full | A magazine blog |
| «Конвас-автомат», Russian Wikipedia | [ru.wikipedia.org](https://ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/Конвас-автомат) | The camera as a type: 290 × 260 × 240 mm, 5.7 kg, turret of three lenses, 60 m magazines, 8 to 32 frames a second. Read in part | Tertiary: the size is cited to *Sovetskaya kinosyomochnaya apparatura* (1974), not seen. Says a Konvas had a regular place in every Vostok's cabin, citing two magazine articles not seen |
| «Восток-2», Russian Wikipedia; spacefacts.de; Wade, Encyclopedia Astronautica, "Vostok 2"; Roscosmos, «Так совершался беспримерный подвиг» (through the Internet Archive) | [ru.wikipedia.org](https://ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/Восток-2); [spacefacts.de](http://www.spacefacts.de/mission/english/vostok-2.htm); [astronautix.com](http://www.astronautix.com/v/vostok2.html) | The 25 h 11 min of the ship read against the pilot's 25 h 18; 88.46 min; the monument's coordinates; 50°51′ N 47°01′30″ E; the US tracking elements (172 by 221 km, 64.80°, 88.40 min); the 1964 drop test. Read in full | Tertiary. spacefacts.de prints Vostok 1's launcher number; Wikipedia's 88.46 min does not fit its own heights |

**Not found**: the State Commission's or OKB-1's report on the flight (so no orbit of the report's
standing, no ascent times, no second for the braking burn); any drawing or photograph of its
cabin; a picture or dimension of the optical instrument (its powers and fields are doc. 175's);
what the BRU-1A was and where it stood; how the Konvas was altered and where it was kept; Titov's height and mass in any document (163 cm
is on biography pages that cite nothing); the third stage engine's number; the weather.

**Not reached**: the Ministry of Defence's publication of the first cosmonauts' files
(60cosmonauts.mil.ru refused the connection), which is where Titov's stature would be;
epizodsspace's own hosts (down; one text read through a mirror); the Internet Archive's copies of
them (it refused the download as automated); Sven Grahn's pages on Vostok's radio and television
(404); NASA NSSDC's record 1961-019A (an error page); Titov's own books beyond the 1961
collection (*Семнадцать космических зорь*, *Голубая моя планета*, *I Am Eagle!*); the film
*Снова к звёздам* (1961); *Первые космические полёты человека* (Academy of Sciences, 1962), the
medical report on both flights.


### Vostok 3 and Vostok 4

Read for [Vostok 3](../missions/ussr/vostok/flights/vostok-3/README.md) and
[Vostok 4](../missions/ussr/vostok/flights/vostok-4/README.md), 13 November 2026. Each page's text
was downloaded and read unless it says otherwise.

| Source | Where | Good for | Watch out for |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Kamanin, *Skrytyi kosmos*, the diary for 1962, 13 July to 18 August | [militera.lib.ru/db/kamanin_np/62.html](http://militera.lib.ru/db/kamanin_np/62.html) | **The fullest record of the days themselves, by a member of the State Commission.** The argument over duration; 26 July: «обзор — 7 градусов, запас рабочего тела на 2–3 часа ориентации»; 27 July: leaving the seat tried in a Tu-104, «все десять операций по освобождению от подвесной системы»; 3 August: «Пятый корабль … уже прошел комплексные испытания … шестой корабль на 1–2 дня может задержать пуск»; 4 August: the drift of 0.06°/s, the planned spin of 0.5°/s dropped; 9 August: the new food, «приборную доску, разработанную Даревским», the code words; 10 August: the washer, the rollout at 21:00; the launch from the bunker: «все ждали отсчета 687», «На 720-й секунде произошло отделение корабля»; «старт Николаева — 11 августа в 11:30 … старт Поповича — 12 августа в 11:08, и назначенные сроки пусков были выдержаны с точностью до 0,5 секунды»; the temperatures (27 to 13 °C; 12 °C, 1.1 atm, 70%; 11 to 12 and then 10 °C and 35%); the callsigns «Сокол» and «Беркут»; «Наблюдаю грозу»; and **both pilots' reports to the State Commission of 16 August as he wrote them down** (6 to 7 g, «в 11:03 угол наклона корабля составил 73 градуса», four times out of the seat, the braking engine heard «в течение 42-х секунд», 8 to 9 g, the patch of 2 by 2 m; Popovich: orienting in the shadow, the VHF noise, pulse 54 to 60, the suit's fan, «Отстрела антенн НАЗа не было»). Read in full for these dates | A diary, edited for print: the pilots' reports are "fragments". **Its 11:08 for Vostok 4's launch contradicts its own record of Nikolayev's report** and every other source. Its clock times at the pad are local where it says so and unclear where it does not |
| "'Group Space Flight' Completed" and "'Group Space Flight' Described", parts 1 and 2, *Flight International*, 23 August (pp. 288 to 289), 30 August (pp. 304 to 305) and 6 September 1962 (pp. 389 to 391) | flightglobal.com/FlightPDFArchive/1962/ (1962 - 1546, 1547, 1638, 1639, 1887, 1888, 1891), through the Internet Archive; read as the PDFs' text layer and, for two pages, as pages | **The Soviet public record in English, within three weeks**: the TASS messages of 12 to 15 August (the orbits as of 12:12 on 12 August: 88.2 and 88.3 min, 227.6 and 234.8 km, 176.7 and 177.9 km, 64°59′ and 64°57′; sleep at 21:00 and 21:30; the landings at 09:55 and 10:01); **the press conference of 21 August**: Keldysh (11:30 and 11:02; 2,600,000 km and 64 orbits; "a special system of cabin loudspeakers and microphones"; "about five tons"), Nikolayev ("no fundamental differences in the structure"; 88.5 min, 183 and 251 km, 65°; the short-wave and VHF sets and the broadcast receiver; the cabin "floodlit for the television sessions"; the Moon, Orion, the entry), Yazdovsky (pulse 120 and 130; 50 and 36 millirad), Popovich ("a very small moon"; the vacuum cleaner; "I landed beside the ship"; "about five kilometres"; "about 200km") | Extracts, in a translation the magazine does not credit. Its own analysis of a "jump" in Vostok 3's orbit is of TASS's announcements, not of the ship |
| Siddiqi, *Challenge to Apollo* (entry under "Read"), pp. 354 to 361, the launch log and Table IB | the same PDF, read as text extracted from it | **The ships' numbers**: Vostok 3, 11 August 1962, 1130, 3KA No. 5; Vostok 4, 12 August, 1102:33, 3KA No. 6. T+687 s; 180.7 by 234.6 km at 64.98°; 179.8 by 236.7 km at 64.95°; Table IB: 4,722 and 4,728 kg, 64 and 48 orbits, 03:22:22 and 02:22:56:27; the descent modules' times from Glushko's encyclopedia (3 d 22 h 9 min 59 s; 2 d 22 h 44 min); the first broadcast on the seventh orbit; "the two cameras mounted in the cabin"; the food; the flask; the filming; 850 km; Mishin on the five kilometres | **Its launch log gives no launch vehicle number for either flight.** Calls Popovich "also thirty-two". Quotes his answer on landing as "like Titov and Gagarin" where *Flight* has "Herman Titov and Andrian Grigoryevich" |
| Chertok, *Rockets and People*, vol. 3 (NASA SP-2009-4110), pp. 204 to 208 | the PDF under "Vostok 2"; read as text for these pages | Korolev's order that the two ships hear each other and the ground hear both, and the work with Bykov's radio men; power for seven days, ten if saved, and Bushuyev's double margin; the washer and the rollout; the three days without rest; the control post; 10 °C; the "thunderstorm" | A memoir. No figure of either ship or orbit |
| V. A. Efimov, «Первая передача телевизионного изображения с космических объектов в системы телевещания "Интервидение" и "Евровидение"», *Tele-Sputnik*, 1996 No. 5 | old.telesputnik.ru/archive/7/article/54.html, through the Internet Archive's copy of April 2021 | **The television, by an engineer of the institute that built it, who ran one of the three receiving stations**: two cameras of 3 kg; 1 kHz lines, 10 frames, 50 kHz; the rebuild to 400 by 400 ordered in mid-1961; «для объектов последующих запусков ("Восток-3" и других) были разработаны, а затем и установлены новые передатчики и передающие камеры»; the re-scanning rig at the stations near Moscow, near Leningrad and in the Crimea; the camera's duty of 15 minutes in an hour and a half; the speech link blocking the receiver on both flights. **Its photograph «Камера "СЕЛИГЕР"» is what the model's cameras are read from** | Written 34 years on. Says the first two flights had only the 100-line system, against Kotelnikov in 1961 (see "Vostok 2"). The photograph is 300 pixels wide, undated and without a scale |
| V. A. Efimov, «Путь к "Востоку" (начало космовидения)», *Elektrosvyaz*, 2007 | [computer-museum.ru/connect/vostok.htm](https://computer-museum.ru/connect/vostok.htm) | The same camera photographed again; «две передающие камеры на дюймовых видиконах ЛИ-23 (массой 3 кг каждая, потребление 12 Вт)»; one camera on the dog in profile and one full face. Read in part, by search | About 1958 to 1960, not 1962 |
| «Селигер (комплекс космического телевидения)», Russian Wikipedia | [ru.wikipedia.org](https://ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/Селигер_(комплекс_космического_телевидения)) | The rebuild named *Yastreb*; the video band of 800 kHz; the stations as NIP-9, NIP-10 and Medvezhyi Ozyora. Read in full | Tertiary: cites the institute's history (*Теория и практика космического телевидения*, 2017), not seen |
| Semenov (ed.), *RKK Energia 1946–1996* (entry under "The launch vehicle"), p. 116 | the same copy | «В ходе полета космонавты вели киносъемку поверхности Земли … А.Г. Николаев снимал поверхность Земли, а П.Р. Попович — линию горизонта и зону терминатора. Кроме того, на борту были установлены кинокамеры для регистрации действий космонавтов»; 94 h and 71 h. Read for this passage | Does not name a camera |
| *Звёздные братья* ("Star Brothers"), the Soviet documentary of the flight, 1962, in colour, as dubbed for export | archive.org, items 61324StarBrotherhoodPt1Vwr and 61334StarBrotherhoodPt2Vwr (Periscope Film); part 2 gone through as frames every four seconds from 4:30 to 15:30, and single frames | The morning of each launch: the orange suits, **the helmets lettered СССР** on both men, the bus, the lift; the received television pictures, square, the pilot full face; the announced orbit drawn on a globe (Апогей 251 км, Перигей 183 км) | **Its cabin scenes are not the flight**: Kamanin records both men filmed in their suits at the seat on 9 August. What looks like the fairing in the pad scenes is the lift's cabin. 640 by 360 pixels; part 1 was not gone through |
| Zak, RussianSpaceWeb, "Joint flight of Vostok-3 and Vostok-4" | [russianspaceweb.com/vostok3.html](https://www.russianspaceweb.com/vostok3.html) | Kamanin and Chertok read together, in English; "3KA No. 5" and "3KA No. 6"; the third stage failure under a Zenit; Popovich's later account of his landing and the 305 km. Read in full | Gives Vostok 4's liftoff as 11:08, from Kamanin. Its photographs are 200 pixels wide |
| McDowell, GCAT launch log | [planet4589.org/space/gcat/tsv/launch/launch.tsv](https://planet4589.org/space/gcat/tsv/launch/launch.tsv); read by search | **The launch vehicles' numbers: E103-23 for Vostok 3, E103-22 for Vostok 4** (and E103-21 for the Zenit-2 lost on 11 December 1961, E103-20 for Kosmos 4), citing Varfolomeyev | Tertiary. Its 08:02:17 for Vostok 4 is 16 s from Siddiqi's |
| spacefacts.de, "Vostok 3" and "Vostok 4"; «Восток-3» and «Восток-4», Russian Wikipedia; "Vostok 3 and 4", English Wikipedia | [spacefacts.de](http://www.spacefacts.de/mission/english/vostok-3.htm); ru.wikipedia.org; en.wikipedia.org | 08:30:00 and 08:02:33 UTC; "Ye10323" and "Ye10322"; the landing places (48°03′32″ N 75°41′02″ E and 48°10′00″ N 71°53′30″ E; 48°02′ N 75°45′ E and 48°09′ N 71°51′ E); 88.33 and 88.39 min; the Vostok 4 capsule "at the NPO Zvezda Museum … modified to represent the Voskhod 2 capsule". Read in full | Tertiary. Russian Wikipedia's Vostok 4 box repeats Vostok 3's orbit and mass. The museum statement has no source |
| visitmuseums.ru, the Nikolayev memorial complex at Shorshely | [visitmuseums.ru](https://visitmuseums.ru/en/museum-a9c879ba-0fb9-4bd6-906e-99dfb23f6c1c.html) | "The exhibition features a landing section of 'Vostok-3'". Read in full | A tourism listing |
| CIA, *Current Intelligence Weekly Summary*, 24 August 1962, "Vostoks III and IV" | archive.org, cia-readingroom-document-cia-rdp79-00927a003700090001-4, its OCR text | How it looked from outside at the time: "the first stage of a rendezvous mission" | Nothing of the hardware |

**Not found**: the State Commission's or OKB-1's report on either flight (so no orbit of a
report's standing, no ascent times but Kamanin's 687 and 720 s, no minute for either braking
burn); the list of alterations Korolev went over with Nikolayev on 10 August; any drawing or
photograph of either cabin as flown; a dimension of the Seliger camera, a dated photograph of the
400-line one, or where either stood; what the fixed film cameras were; the name of the hand-held
one; the radio frequencies of either ship; anything changed in the cable mast after Vostok 2;
either pilot's height; why Vostok 4's cabin went cold; the weather beyond the forecast of the
11th (an anticyclone, clear, 6 to 7 m/s).

**Not reached, or not read**: Hall and Shayler, *The Rocket Men* (no copy was to be had); the
Soviet collections of the newspapers' texts for these flights and *Первый групповой космический
полёт* (Academy of Sciences, 1964), which are on epizodsspace, whose hosts did not answer;
*Pravda* itself for 12 to 22 August 1962 (the TASS texts were read only in *Flight*'s English);
the interview with Popovich in *Novosti Kosmonavtiki* that RussianSpaceWeb cites; Sven Grahn's
pages (404); Nikolayev's *Встретимся на орбите* and Popovich's *Вылетаю утром*; Kriss, *ОКБ МЭИ и
«Восток»* (2011), on the television's radio link; the museum at Shorshely's own record of the
Vostok 3 descent module, and NPP Zvezda's of Vostok 4's.


Read again for Vostok 3's launch site and flight deck, 16 November 2026, each checked against its text before a figure
went in:

| Source | Where | Good for | Watch out for |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Kamanin, *Skrytyi kosmos*, the diary for 1962 (the entry above), 22 June, 3, 10, 12 to 16 August | [militera.lib.ru/db/kamanin_np/62.html](http://militera.lib.ru/db/kamanin_np/62.html), its text downloaded and searched | **The fire**: «При третьем пуске [Зенита] — отказала первая ступень, и ракета упала в 300-х метрах от старта. Одна из четырех боковушек осталась на старте и сгорела ... восстановление займет около месяца» (22 June). The «Львов» bus ordered for the cosmonauts (3 August); the rollout at 21:00 on the 9th (10 August); **the morning of the 11th** (12 August): the State Commission at 7:30 «в новом помещении по соседству с бункером», the forecast «антициклон, ясно, скорость ветра 6–7 метров в секунду», Korolev's post «в 10 метрах от ракеты ... небольшой бетонированный козырек», «В 11:30 подъехал специальный автобус с двумя космонавтами, одетыми в скафандры», the report to Smirnov, the lift; the 687 and 720 s and the 0.5 s (14 August); 26 °C and 50 % before liftoff; the 65th orbit; Nikolayev's report (16 August) | Its clock at the pad is the range's, two hours ahead of Moscow's (derived: an 11:30 bus cannot be at the 11:30 liftoff). Says nothing of the morning of the 12th but that it "differed little" |
| Siddiqi, *Challenge to Apollo* (the entry above), pp. 355 to 360, Tables IA and IB | [history.nasa.gov/SP-4408pt1.pdf](https://history.nasa.gov/SP-4408pt1.pdf), its text extracted | **1 June 1962**: "from the launch pad at site 1 ... the rocket landed 300 meters from the launch site, while one of the strap-ons remained at the pad, seriously damaging the structure as a result of a fire" (p. 355); T+687 s and "nominal" (p. 356); sleep at 2200 (p. 357); "as close as five kilometers", n. 22 "from five to six and a half" (p. 358); the module's times from Glushko, n. 29 (p. 360) | Table IB gives no period |
| NOAA Integrated Surface Database, 1962: stations 35849099999 (Kazaly), 35953099999 (Zhosaly), 38062099999 (Kzyl-Orda), 35746099999 (Aralsk), 38069099999 (Shieli), 11 and 12 August | [noaa-global-hourly-pds](https://noaa-global-hourly-pds.s3.amazonaws.com/1962/35849099999.csv) (and the others by their numbers) | Every row issue #15 quoted for the two days checked against the files: no cloud at Kazaly, Zhosaly or Kzyl-Orda on the 11th through noon UTC, the wind 020° to 050° at 1 to 10 m/s, 25 to 33 °C; one okta of cumulus at Aralsk at 09:00; on the 12th clear, 070° to 090° at 3 to 9 m/s, 30 to 34 °C | Stations 74 to 220 km from the pad, none at the range; Kazaly reports 20 km as its furthest visibility |
| *Большая советская энциклопедия*, 3rd edition (1969–1978), «Восток (космический корабль)» | [booksite.ru/FullText/1/001/008/006/782.htm](https://booksite.ru/FullText/1/001/008/006/782.htm), its text | **The orbits Siddiqi gives, from a Soviet reference**: Vostok 3 180.7 by 234.6 km, Vostok 4 179.8 by 236.7 km (and Vostok 2 178 by 244, Vostok 5 181 by 222.1, Vostok 6 183 by 231); the landings "in the region of Karkaralinsk" and "of the settlement of Atasu", Karaganda oblast; "the least distance between the ships about 5 km"; the group flight 70 h 28 min | No period for any of them. Vostok 3's 94 h 10 min is the ship's, not the pilot's |
| SRTM, 90 m, through the OpenTopoData API | api.opentopodata.org, dataset srtm90m | The landing grounds' heights: 738 and 693 m at Vostok 3's two printed places, 601 and 592 m at Vostok 4's | Of the year 2000; the printed places themselves differ by 5 km |
| Russian Wikipedia, «Восток-3» and «Восток-4» (the entry above) | their wikitext, downloaded | 88.33 min beside 180.7 by 234.6 km; the landing places | No source for the period; «Восток-4»'s box repeats Vostok 3's orbit |

Read for [Vostok 5](../missions/ussr/vostok/flights/vostok-5/README.md) and
[Vostok 6](../missions/ussr/vostok/flights/vostok-6/README.md), 13 November 2026. Each page's text
was downloaded and read unless it says otherwise.

**Not found**: either pilot's height, or Tereshkova's weight; a dimension, a mass or a photograph
on display of the SK-2; the name of either ship's cine camera; what the binoculars, photometer,
filters and experiment containers were; what "some additional equipment" of May 1963 was; any
OKB-1 or State Commission report on either flight (so no ascent times at all, no minute for either
braking burn, no account of why Vostok 5's orbit was low); what a woman's seat differed in; the
weather on either launch day as a figure; the survey behind either landing place.

**Not reached, or not read**: Glushko's *Kosmonavtika: Entsiklopediya* (1985), from which the later
orbit and the descent modules' times are said to come; Golovanov's *Korolev*; Ponomareva's and
Yazdovsky's memoirs; «Второй групповой космический полёт» (Academy of Sciences); *Novosti
Kosmonavtiki*; the press conference of 25 June in Russian; part 2 of Siddiqi's *Spaceflight*
article; zvezda-npp.ru; Encyclopedia Astronautica's suit pages; the Russian edition of Abramov and
Skoog (2005); *Flight International* for June and July 1963; epizodsspace's own hosts.

### The published orbit as mean elements (issue #8)

Read 16 November 2026 for `src/world/meanelements.ts` (docs/agents/world.md). The two 1959 papers were read from the
NASA ADS scans, the equations from page images at 260 dpi.

| Source | Where | Good for | Watch out for |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Kozai, Y., "The motion of a close earth satellite", *Astronomical Journal* 64, 367-377 (1959), doi:10.1086/107957 | [ADS 1959AJ.....64..367K](https://articles.adsabs.harvard.edu/pdf/1959AJ.....64..367K) | Eq. 1 (the potential, A2 = 3/2 J2 R^2), eq. 10 (the first-order short-periodic terms in a, e, w, i, the node and e M), eq. 11 and 12 (their averages over M), eq. 13 (r and the argument of latitude, his second derivation: the check), p. 372 (the secular rates, a0 and a-bar, eq. 14), eqs. 25 to 31 (long-periodic terms, the results), p. 377 (small e: use r and v + w) | His a-bar (eq. 14) places the satellite (eqs. 13, 31) and is not the average of the osculating a; p. 371 prints da = -a0 (...) where carrying it through eq. 13 for a circle over the equator wants +a0. The long-periodic terms divide by 4 - 5 sin^2 i |
| Brouwer, D., "Solution of the problem of artificial satellite theory without drag", *AJ* 64, 378-397 (1959) | [ADS 1959AJ.....64..378B](https://articles.adsabs.harvard.edu/pdf/1959AJ.....64..378B) | Section 9, pp. 393-394 only: a'' and n0, the secular rates of l'', g'' and h'' (to first order Kozai's, with Kozai's a0 for a''), the long-periodic terms' (1 - 5 cos^2 I) divisor, and that g + l carries no 1/e | The rest of the paper (the von Zeipel transformation, its short-periodic terms) was not read |
| Jason-3 Products Handbook, issue 1.5, 17 September 2018 (CNES, EUMETSAT, NASA, NOAA) | [ospo.noaa.gov hdbk_j3.pdf](https://www.ospo.noaa.gov/Products/documents/hdbk_j3.pdf), Tables 5 and 6, p. 17 | A published mean orbit with its nodal period and node rate beside it: a 7,714.432 km, e 0.000098, i 66.042 deg, w 90 deg; nodal period 6,745.7605 s, inertial nodal rate -2.07707 deg/day, 127 revolutions in 9.91564 days | "Mean classical orbit elements": the theory is not named. That the first-order nodal period of a = a0 comes within 2 ms says the axis is the averaged one (Kozai's a-bar would be 2.2 km less, 2.8 s) |
| AVISO, "Sentinel-3 orbit" | [aviso.altimetry.fr](https://www.aviso.altimetry.fr/en/missions/current-missions/sentinel-3a/orbit.html) | a 7,177.926540 km, e 0.001148, i 98.65 deg, w 90 deg, nodal period 100.987 min, 385 revolutions in 27 days: a Sun-synchronous orbit with its period | Does not say the elements are mean ones; the inclination is given to 0.01 degree |
| Brykov, A. V., memoir of NII-4's ballistics group and Sputnik 1 (RGANTD f. 227, op. 1, d. 6), at second hand from the research comment on issue #8 | [rvsn.info](https://rvsn.info/library/docs/doc_1_1056.html) | The period found from tracked passes and the height from the period: why the published period is read as a nodal one (`CONVENTION.kind`, estimated) | Sputnik 1 in 1957, not Vostok; a memoir |
| Dolginov, Sh. Sh., Nalivayko, V. N., et al., *Catalog of measured and calculated values of the stress modulus of the geomagnetic field along the orbit of the Cosmos 49 satellite*, part 1 (IZMIRAN, Moscow 1967); translated as NASA CR-99055 (Techtran, 1968), text and table headings only | [NTRS 19690006212](https://ntrs.nasa.gov/citations/19690006212), the translation's p. 5 | "The geodesic coordinates and altitude of the satellite were calculated relative to a biaxial ellipsoid with the following characteristics: large half axis a = 6378.178 km [as the scan reads], compression = 0.00335238918", the heights in the Soviet orbit catalogue it was given, to a kilometre: read 16 November 2026, for `CONVENTION.radius` (the ellipsoid under the apsis, assumed; tests/apsis.test.mjs) | Cosmos 49 in 1964, a catalogue's instantaneous heights, not a published perigee and apogee of a crewed flight: support for the reading, not this unit's statement. The flattening printed is near Krasovsky's 1/298.3; the half axis is not Krasovsky's 6,378.245 km, and the scan is poor |

### The air in orbit and the drag in it (issue #31)

Read 16 November 2026 for `src/sim/atmosphere.ts` (`meanFreePath`) and `src/sim/orbital.ts` (`orbitDrag`, `rarefaction`),
and Vostok's figure (vostok-3ka.ts `SHIP_RAREFIED`).

| Source | Where | Good for | Watch out for |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| *U.S. Standard Atmosphere, 1976* (entry under "The flight as flown"), Table II | [NTRS 19770009539](https://ntrs.nasa.gov/api/citations/19770009539/downloads/19770009539.pdf), pp. 92 and 93 (the scan's pages 107 and 108) | The mean free path from 86 to 200 km: 1.17e-2 m at 86 km, 0.142 at 100, 3.31 at 120, 33 at 150, 240 at 200 | Its first column is headed "H" and holds the geometric heights |
| King-Hele, D. G., *The Prediction of Satellite Lifetimes*, RAE Technical Report 87030, May 1987 | DTIC ADA189710 | §9.1, eq. 20: δ = F S C_D / m, S "its mean cross-sectional area perpendicular to the direction of motion", "a value of 2.2 is recommended for perigee heights of 150-400 km" (after Cook 1965); §8: the coefficient falls below 130 km and is about half its free-molecule value by 90 km | A satellite's figure in general, not the Vostok's |
| Wilmoth, R. G., Mitcheltree, R. A. and Moss, J. N., "Low-density aerodynamics of the Stardust sample return capsule", *J. Spacecraft and Rockets* 36(3), 436-441, 1999 | NTRS 20040105522 | Kn = λ / D; continuum below Kn 0.01 and free-molecular above 10 "as general guidelines", 0.001 and 10 taken for Stardust; the bridge C = C_cont + (C_fm - C_cont) sin^2 π(a1 + a2 log10 Kn) (eqs. 1 and 2) | The constants a1 = 3/8 and a2 = 1/8 are not printed: they are what the limits 0.001 and 10 give (derived here) |
| Kamanin, *Skrytyi kosmos*, the diary for 4 August 1962 | [militera.lib.ru/db/kamanin_np/62.html](http://militera.lib.ru/db/kamanin_np/62.html) | Rauschenbach to the State Commission: after orienting the ship turns at 0.06°/s, once in 1.8 hours; the pilot as a rule spins it to 0.5°/s, once in twelve minutes, so the Sun warms it evenly; Korolev let Vostoks 3 and 4 do without the spin | Read by search, at second hand through a research note; the print page was not had |
| Siddiqi, *Challenge to Apollo* (entry under "Vostok"), pp. 195, 197, 293, 357, 358, 368 | the same PDF | The ship's 4.4 m and 2.43 m and the 2.3 m ball; the instrument module "two cone-shaped compartments with their blunt ends facing each other"; the pilots' orientations by hand; Bykovsky's "thermal roll mode at one revolution per eight minutes" (p. 368) | The cones' end diameters are in nothing read: the model's are scaled off a cutaway |
